### C-37 A trial of CPU-heavy steps on TACC
**H** for step (0), the PI's; then **M** · Pod: no; **TACC: yes** (rules below) · Depends: C-37a for steps (1) to (4); step (3) reads stage H1's files, which are built · asked for by the PI on 2026-11-16 ("at some point, i will want to move cpu-intensive work to tacc ... make a card with a trial to do that in the near term-ish. i have tacc credits")
Files: `profiles/` (one new host profile for the TACC system, only if step (4) needs it to run a step), `scripts/tacc/` (new: Slurm job scripts and a driver for the trial), `RUNBOOK.md` (a new section, only for what was run), one measurement note with its directory. No change under `src/` in this card: what the code would need is reported, not done.
What TACC is, from its public user guides read on 2026-11-16 (docs.tacc.utexas.edu: Lonestar6 and Stampede3 guides, both "last update September 18, 2026"; the MFA page; TACC's containers tutorial). None of it is measured by us; step (1) checks what matters.
- The Texas Advanced Computing Center at UT Austin: batch clusters under Slurm. **Lonestar6** is the system for UT System researchers, with allocations through TxRAS; 560 compute nodes of 128 cores each (two AMD EPYC 7763), 256 GB of memory, 288 GB of node-local `/tmp` that is purged at the end of each job. **Stampede3** is allocated through NSF ACCESS (nodes of 48 to 112 cores, 128 to 256 GB, three nodes with 4 TB). Frontera was retired in 2026 and Horizon is in early operations (news reports; not checked on TACC's own pages).
- Cost: **a whole node is billed, 1 SU per node-hour** in Lonestar6's `normal` queue (1 to 64 nodes a job, 48 hours at most, 20 jobs a user); `development` is 2 hours at most at the same rate; every job is billed at least 0.25 hours. `taccinfo` shows a project's balance. Startup allocations are 500 SUs and research allocations up to 100,000 (a search result, not TACC's own page: check in step (0)).
- Files: `$HOME` 10 GB; `$WORK` 1 TB and 3,000,000 files, not purged, not backed up; `$SCRATCH` without quota, **files not accessed for ten days are purged**. Login nodes are for file management and data movement only; all computing is a batch or interactive job.
- Containers: Apptainer through `module load tacc-apptainer`, **on compute nodes only**; an existing `.sif` runs with `apptainer exec --cleanenv`. Many serial tasks in one job: PyLauncher, or Slurm job arrays.
- Login: **every SSH login needs a new MFA code** (an authenticator app or a text message). The guides say nothing about scripted logins or reused connections.
- Against the pod (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-16, `lscpu`): four Xeon E7-4850 v3 at 2.2 GHz, 56 cores and 112 threads for every user, of which we may use 40 or 56 threads. No TACC file system is mounted on the pod (*measured*, `mount`). So one Lonestar6 node has more than twice the pod's cores, all ours for the job's length, with no 300 CPU-minute limit.
- What that would cost (*derived* from our measured rates, assuming a Lonestar6 core does what a pod thread does, which step (3) measures): the full Pfam scan of one phase-2 stage, 9,961 to 12,428 CPU-minutes (*estimate*, card C-36), is 1.3 to 1.6 node-hours, so 8 to 10 SUs for the six stages; `rM.2`'s clustering, 14,650 CPU-minutes with a peak of 178 GB (*measured*), is about 2 node-hours of CPU and fits one node's memory.
Do:
(0) **The PI**: which TACC system and project the credits are on, and their balance (`taccinfo`, or the portal); the MFA device is the PI's, so say how an agent may work there: (a) the PI opens one SSH connection and the agent's commands reuse it (ask TACC whether that is allowed; the guides do not say), (b) the agent writes the job scripts and the PI submits them, or (c) TACC's job API, if the project may use it. Until this is answered an agent does not log in.
Answered in part on 2026-11-16 (the PI: "i have tacc credits for all services, use the most appropriate"). **The system is Lonestar6** (the orchestrator's choice, from the published queue tables): its `normal` queue gives 128 cores for 1 SU per node-hour, against 48 cores for 1 SU (`skx`), 80 for 1.5 (`icx`) and 112 for 2 (`spr`) on Stampede3, and its 256 GB a node holds a clustering of `rM.2`'s size (178 GB peak, *measured*); the image is x86-64, so Vista (ARM) is out; Horizon is in early operations and has no guide we have read. Kept for later, not for this trial: Stampede3's three `nvdimm` nodes (4 TB of memory, 4 SUs per node-hour) for a clustering that outgrows 256 GB. The project name is read from `/usr/local/etc/taccinfo` at the first login. **How an agent logs in: by SSH, option (a)** (the PI: "i dont know the access, but prefer ssh"): the PI opens one multiplexed connection from the laptop with password and token code (`ssh -fN -M -S ~/.ssh/cm-ls6 -o ControlPersist=12h <TACC username>@ls6.tacc.utexas.edu`), and the agent runs its commands through that socket (`ssh -S ~/.ssh/cm-ls6 ls6.tacc.utexas.edu <command>`), never with a password, a code or a key of its own; when the connection has closed the agent stops and says so. The guide forbids running `ssh-keygen` on Lonestar6. Before that first login the PI needs: the TACC username (TACC's own account, not the UT EID login), an MFA pairing made in the TACC accounts portal, and membership of a project with a Lonestar6 allocation (the guide: "You must be added to a Lonestar6 allocation in order to have access").
(1) A probe, on a login node and in one `development` job: the balance; `module spider apptainer`; the node's CPU model and flags; whether a compute node reaches the internet; whether `systemd-run --user --scope` works there (`run-step` opens a memory-capped scope with it on the pod) and what Slurm gives instead; the quotas; the transfer rate of a 10 GB file from the pod to `$SCRATCH` (rsync over the PI's connection, or Globus).
(2) The image: copy `bio-box_faec1f4.sif` to `$WORK`, check its sha256 against RUNBOOK §0, and run `tests/r0` and `tests/r1` in it in a `development` job, with the tree's `src` on `PYTHONPATH` as on the pod.
(3) **The same bytes on another host** (AGENTS §4.3; the stage plan's cross-host check D-05): scan stage H1's proteins with Pfam 38.2 on one `normal` node, from copies of H1's `protein_seq` file and the Pfam file, with the same plan, shards and merge as the pod's build (`stage domains plan|shard|merge`, the shards as tasks of one job), into `$SCRATCH`. Compare the merged `l1_domains` file with the data root's by sha256 (`8ad24980c97cde1c7da52904073060edccb7bf60613ac27b3c6d0e1da2bf10e0`, card C-24's list) and each shard record with the root's. Record wall time, CPU-minutes, SUs, and CPU-minutes per million residues against the pod's 7.18 (*measured*, card C-24). Nothing produced at TACC goes into the data root in this card.
(4) Report: the measured rate and SUs; from them the cost of the six phase-2 scans, of a release's clustering and of stage S5's scan; what the code would need to run a stage's heavy steps there (a host profile, `run-step` under Slurm without a systemd scope, a launcher for shards and batches in place of `build-stage`'s own processes, moving inputs and results by manifest and sha256, what happens at the 48-hour limit and at the ten-day purge); and the question for the PI that a real use raises: results made on another host entering the data root (AGENTS §7.1 says outputs are produced only through `run-step` in the image on the pod).
Rules at TACC for this card: only the PI's project and only after step (0); **at most 20 SUs in all**, each job's request stated before it is submitted and its charge after; nothing but file management on login nodes; no MFA code, password or key is stored, asked for or worked around by an agent; nothing is kept only on `$SCRATCH` that is not also on the pod; only public genome data and this repository's code go there; AGENTS §8 holds (no fetches from TACC nodes except the listed sources).
Accept: the measurement note with steps (1) to (4); the sha256 comparison of step (3), equal or not, stated; the SUs used.

**Cards C-38 to C-45, and C-48: metagenomic sources beyond the ladder.**
Written on 2026-11-16 at the PI's word ("make cards for all the dbs that i'm missing and think thru how to stage importing them"). The survey and the reasoning are `planning/sources_metagenomic_survey.md` (§2 the sources, §4 the staging). Rules common to all eight: **no byte is fetched from a source until its entry is in `sources.yaml` with the PI's approval** (AGENTS §8), so each card's first step is the proposed entry (exact URL form, files and sizes, the provider's checksum, licence and terms as the provider states them), handed back for approval; no email address, name or project detail goes in any request; a set under JGI's terms is flagged `redistributable = false` and stays local; nothing is built in the data root by these cards (a build is a later card each); every published count is named with its source and checked against the files once they are here.
Amended 2026-11-16 (DEC-50; the PI: "agents can read remaingin providers public pages. they can also fetch data as needed including but not limited to zenomdo", and on JGI "i have login and permissinos, but defer ... remind me later"): for cards C-39 to C-44 the entry is no longer handed back. The agent reads the provider's public pages, writes the entry into `sources.yaml` (exact URL, files, sizes, the provider's checksum, licence or terms as stated, and `approved: under DEC-50`) **before** it fetches, then fetches and checks the checksum. Still in force: no name, email address or project detail in any request; only pages and files open without a login; nothing fetched is executed; each card's limits on scratch and threads. **Any licence may be used** (DEC-51; the PI: "use all data, even restrictive license, bc we're academic/non-profit and have rights to tis"): terms that restrict reuse do not stop a fetch or a use; the entry still records them, with `redistributable: false` where they limit redistribution, as a record for the day a release is shared. **No agent tries a JGI source** (IMG/VR, IMG/PR, IMG/M, GOLD): they wait for the PI, and each card says what it could not do without them. Four entries were approved by the PI directly and are in `sources.yaml`: `uhgv`, `gsv_atlas`, `spire_tables`, `ncbi_metagenome_assembly_summary` (`planning/measurements/2026-11-16_proposed_sources.md`).
Amended 2026-11-18 (PI logged into JGI/GOLD in Chrome, accepted the JGI Data Utilization Policy, and asked to enumerate all JGI datasets, pull the largest useful collections and environmental metadata onto `wilkcomp02`, and card the staged harvest of the rest as the DuckDB corpus progresses): nine new `sources.yaml` entries are approved (`jgi_gold_downloads`, `jgi_img_m_tables`, `jgi_img_vr`, `jgi_img_pr`, `jgi_img_umag`, `jgi_iuig`, `jgi_inovirus`, `jgi_gems_nersc`, `gvmags_v2`), streaming onto `/stor/work/Wilke/if2453/all-the-data/jgi_20261118/`; cards **C-48** (`C-48a` to `C-48e`) govern locking those archives, joining their environmental metadata into DuckDB dimension/crosswalk tables, and progressively staging and building the viral, plasmid, MAG/isolate, tape-purged JGI, and unbinned metagenomic datasets into the rolling release line.

### C-38 Design: importing at a stated depth, and annotating on the hit
**H** (a design note first, for the PI to decide), then **M** cards from it · Depends: none; it uses the measurements of C-37 and C-42 as they arrive · the survey's §4
Files: `planning/design/C-38_import_depths.md` (new), nothing else
Why: unbinned metagenomic contigs cannot be annotated in full (the survey's §4.1: about 20 times the cost of calling genes; 1.2 years of the pod for SPIRE's 16 Tbp, *derived*), and they do not fit the pod's local disk (0.71 GB per Gbp at the depth of called genes, *derived* from H1). Today a stage is all or nothing.
Do, as a design note in the form of `planning/design/B-47_inferred_domains.md`: (1) a stage's **depth** (stored; called: genes, proteins, tapes, CRISPR arrays; clustered; annotated) as a recorded property, and what a release, a search pack, `release verify` and the manifest do with stages of different depth; (2) **annotation on the hit**: a search over called proteins, then Pfam on the proteins within N genes of each hit, where those rows live (AGENTS §3.10: a stage never rewrites a file, so a new partition kind and its scope rule), how a reader tells a protein without domains from one never scanned; (3) families without Pfam: a linear clustering of all proteins and neighbour statistics on cluster tokens, with what is lost against the cascade we use; (4) the filter at ingest (a least length or gene count), with the dropped bases in the ledger; (5) nucleotides by reference for unbinned contigs (proteins, coordinates and arrays kept; the sequence refetchable by source, sample, contig and sha256): what breaks in the spec's invariants and in the tools that read sequence; (6) where a corpus of several TB lives (a larger footprint on `/stor/scratch`, TACC storage, or the depth-1 part held apart from the data root); (7) the stopping rule by habitat (new NR50 families per Gbp). Each with options, what was measured, a recommendation, and the questions only the PI can answer.
Amended 2026-11-16 (DEC-49; the PI: "keep 1-4 , i will think about 5-9, but i think i'd like to keep nucleotides too (5)"): **the note designs items (1) to (4), which are decided in principle.** Item (5) is dropped as a proposal: nucleotides are kept, so the note plans storage at the full figure and only records what the reference form would have saved. Items (6) and (7), and the survey's ideas (7) and (8), are the PI's to think about: the note lays out the options and what each needs measured, and recommends nothing that depends on them. The note may be written now; it does not wait for cards C-37 or C-42, and says which of its numbers they will replace.
Done 2026-11-17 (STATUS row C-38): the note is `planning/design/C-38_import_depths.md`; its fourteen questions were confirmed as recommended by DEC-54; its follow-on cards C-38a to C-38h follow below.
Accept: the note; its list of questions for the PI; the cards that follow from the recommendation, as text for this file.

### C-39 Viruses from metagenomes, as vOTU representatives: inputs and overlap
**M** · Pod: yes (reads; writes only under `/tmp/if2453/s3/`) · Depends: the PI's approval of the source entries (step 1) · widens stage S4 of `STAGES.md`

Sources (published counts, the survey's §2): UHGV (873,994 genomes, 168,536 vOTUs; human gut); the Global Soil Virus Atlas (616,935 genomes, 38,508 vOTUs); IMG/VR v4's vOTUs with a high-quality member (231,408 of 8.7 million; JGI terms); VIRE (over 1.7 million genomes) only for the overlap question.
Do: (1) the proposed `sources.yaml` entries, for approval. (2) After approval, fetch the vOTU representative sequences and their tables (habitat, host, quality, genetic code where given). (3) Inputs as stage `V1` (or one stage per source if their terms differ: say which), kind `virus`, in the form of card C-26 part 1. ### C-45 Decisions on what stays out
**H** (the PI's) · Depends: none
**Amended 2026-11-20 (DEC-59): nothing here "stays out" for good.** The goal is a complete catalog, and what is not taken now is deferred and listed in `planning/CATALOG_GOAL.md` §4. Question (2) below is read as "in, later"; (4) stands. **Answered 2026-11-20 (DEC-60 (2)): (1) RNA viruses are in, by design card C-50; (3) protein-only catalogues "can wait" (deferred).**
1. **RNA viruses** (LucaProt's 161,979 species, Serratus' RdRps, RVMT): everything built assumes DNA; taking them means a gene caller and a token model for RNA genomes. In, later, or out?
2. **Strain-level sets** (AllTheBacteria's 2.44 million isolates; the full MAG sets behind GlobDB's species, such as the mOTUs database's 2.83 million MAGs): they multiply copies of species we hold. The stage plan's S6 already takes every GTDB genome; take these too, only for chosen species, or not at all?
3. **Protein-only catalogues** (MGnify's proteins, the global gene catalogues): proteins without their neighbours, so they serve homology and not context. A search-only pack beside the corpus, or out?
4. **The JGI sets** (IMG/VR, IMG/PR, IMG/M): held local and never redistributed, as the plan says for S4. Confirm for each as its card comes up.

Files: `planning/measurements/` (one note), `planning/design/C-49_tiers.md` (new, a proposal only), `profiles/` only if a profile for the host is proposed (not used by any build in this card)
Why: the data root is on the pod's local disk (1.1 TB in all, cleared at a reboot), its durable copy and the source archives are on the group's `/stor/work` (1.2 TB of ours, where the footprint draft says under 50 GB), and SPIRE alone at 5 kb is about 3 TB at depth `called`. The PI offers a NAS: "i have a nas called tower, and as we grow, maybe tranches can move there?". Read from the PI's laptop on 2026-11-20 (*measured*, `df`, `nproc`, `free`): the array is 32 TB with 18 TB free, a cache disk of 954 GB with 242 GB free, 8 cores, 31 GB of memory. So it can hold tranches and cannot build or cluster them (the pod has 112 cores and a 200 GB cap per job). It is on the PI's home network, which the pod cannot reach; the laptop reaches both.
Do: (1) **What can live there.** For each kind of file (source archives; a stage's files; a release's files; packs; the durable tars), say whether moving it to `tower` breaks anything: which commands read it and how often, whether a search or `nbr` needs it on the machine that computes, and what a release that lists files in two places would need from the manifest and the profile (AGENTS §3.9: the manifest is the record). Propose tiers in `planning/design/C-49_tiers.md` (for example: working root on the pod; older releases' large files and stages outside the newest release on `tower`; `tower` as the second durable copy), with what each costs in transfers. No code. (2) **How data would get there**, with one small measured transfer for each route that needs no new credential: laptop as relay (`rsync` pod → laptop → `tower`), rate in MB/s both hops (*measured*). Routes that need the PI: `tower` pulling from the pod through `wilkcomp03` with a key of its own. Not a route: anything installed or listening on the pod (AGENTS §7.5, §8). For each: rate, what must stay switched on, how a transfer is verified (sha256 on both ends) and resumed. From the rate, the days to move 100 GB, 1 TB and 3 TB (*derived*). (3) **For the PI to set up or decide**, listed and not done by the agent: which share and directory on `tower`; a key for the pull route, restricted to read the data root and the backup directory; whether data whose terms say "not redistributed" (the JGI sets, DEC-51) may sit on a personal NAS; what happens to the copy on `/stor/work` once `tower` holds one (agents do not delete under `/stor`).
Accept: the note with the measured rates and the capacities; the tier proposal; the list for the PI. Nothing of the corpus is copied to `tower` by this card beyond the test files, which are removed.
Done 2026-11-20 (STATUS row C-49): `scripts/orchestrator/relay_to_tower.sh` with its tests, the tier proposal `planning/design/C-49_tiers.md` (built stages cannot live only on `tower`; it is the second durable copy and a home for archives, inputs and lists), the note `planning/measurements/2026-11-20_tower_relay.md`. Owed by the orchestrator: the script's first real use and its measured rate, and the rest of the first mirror's rows in the note.
First measurements and the PI's answers (2026-11-20, the orchestrator, from the laptop; the card's design half is still to do): (a) **the PI: data whose terms say "not redistributed" may sit on `tower`** ("3. yes"); **"pulling via laptop is fine"**; the share, the directory and a key wait on this card's report. (b) The laptop has 26 GiB free (*measured*, `df`), so a relay must stream and never land a file there: `scp -3 -F <config>` with a config naming the pod (through `wilkcomp03`) and `tower` does that in one command. (c) One file, one stream, pod → laptop → `tower`: `bio-box_faec1f4.sif`, 821,764,096 bytes in 84.75 s, **9.70 MB/s** (*measured*; *derived*: bytes / seconds), written to a temporary directory in the share `research-bulk` and read back there with sha256 `d67300aa…f098a`, equal to the pod's; the file and directory were then removed. At that rate (*derived*): 100 GB in 2.9 hours, 1 TB in 29 hours, 3 TB in 3.6 days, with the laptop awake and on the home network throughout. Which hop limits it, and whether parallel streams add, is not measured. (d) `tower`'s shares (*measured*, read-only): `research-bulk` has the cache off (writes go to the array: three data disks with 1.8, 7.3 and 8.8 TB free) and already holds other projects' bulk data; `research` prefers the 954 GB cache disk (242 GB free), which a large copy would fill. The orchestrator's suggestion to the PI: `/mnt/user/research-bulk/bio-box/` with `root_backups/` (the durable tars, first), `sources/` (archives after their stages are built) and `tranches/` (stage files moved off the pod, once the tier design says how a release names them).

### C-50 RNA viruses: sources, gene calling and tokens for RNA genomes
**H** (a design card; the PI decides its questions) · Depends: none · DEC-60 (2), card C-45 question (1)
Files: `planning/design/C-50_rna_viruses.md` (new); no code
Why: RNA viruses are in scope (DEC-60 (2)). Everything built assumes DNA genomes: the gene caller's modes (`single`, `meta`, `gv-meta`), `genome_input.kind` (`prokaryote`, `virus`, `plasmid`), Pfam on proteins called from one strand and frame model, tapes of genes in order on a contig. RNA virus genomes are short, often segmented, often one or two polyproteins, and many sets are RdRp-centred contigs from metatranscriptomes.
Do: (1) the sources (LucaProt's 161,979 species, Serratus' RdRp contigs, RVMT, RefSeq's RNA viruses, others the literature names since): for each, what it holds (genomes, segments, contigs, proteins only), size, licence, how it overlaps the others, and what a non-redundant take is and what it leaves (DEC-59 (3)). (2) What a stage of RNA genomes needs: the genome kind, segments as contigs of one genome, the gene caller (what the pinned tools give on RNA virus genomes against the providers' own calls, measured on a sample; polyproteins and their mature peptides; frameshifts), genetic codes, and what a neighbourhood means on a 10 kb genome. (3) What search and tokens need: Pfam's coverage of RNA virus proteins, RdRp profiles, whether tokens by domain family carry over. (4) Questions for the PI, each with a recommendation, and the cards that would follow.
Accept: the design note; the PI's answers recorded as a decision row.

### B-47b Copy measured Pfam rows instead of scanning again (DEC-35 (2))
**M** · Pod: yes (tests, and one plan in a scratch view; read-only on the data root) · Depends: B-47 (question 2, answered by DEC-35) · Design: `planning/design/B-47_inferred_domains.md` §2b, §5 · copied here from the note's §10 on 2026-11-15, with the orchestrator's additions marked
Files: `src/biobox/l1/domains.py` (the plan leaves out proteins measured in an earlier stage with the same HMM sha256, cutoff and tool versions; the merge copies their rows; the plan names the files copied from, with sha256), `src/biobox/stage.py` (passes the root's other stages, as `--seq-stages`), `src/biobox/cli.py` only for the option on the `stage domains` actions, `RUNBOOK.md` only where a command it shows gains the option, tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, one measurement note
Do, test first: two stages sharing proteins, built in either order, give byte-identical `l1_domains` files; a copied row equals the row of a full scan of that protein (`hmmsearch` binary as oracle); a source scanned with another HMM file or pyhmmer version is not copied. The scanned and copied counts go in the plan and shard records only (`domain_scan`, never in a manifest, not in the ledger).
Added by the orchestrator: (1) "measured in an earlier stage" is decided from that stage's scanned proteins and its completed domain step (its plan and run record), never from the presence of rows: a protein scanned with no hit has no row and is measured. (2) A source stage counts only when its recorded HMM sha256, cutoff, Z and tool versions equal this scan's; say which recorded fields are compared. (3) The shards and the CPU estimate before launch are made from the residues actually scanned. (4) A stage that shares no protein with a named stage, and a stage built with no other stage named, give `l1_domains` and ledger files byte-identical to today's; report whether the fixture's manifest hash changes (expected: it does not). (5) Bounded memory (AGENTS §4.1, §4.2): the set of already measured proteins is found by a streamed or sorted anti-join on `protein_uid`, not a Python set of a stage's proteins; a memory test on the 100× synthetic store with the peak RSS stated. (6) When several named stages measured a protein, the source is the first in the order given, and the rows are equal whichever is taken (test it). (7) Rows that a later card marks as inferred (`inferred_from`, card B-47c) are not measured and are never copied: leave the place for that filter and say where it is.
Accept: the tests; the measurement note, from the pod, read-only on the data root and with no scan run: the plan of stage H1 in a scratch view that names S1 and P1 to P6, its count of proteins to copy against the 5,612 distinct proteins H1 shares with them (*measured*, card C-24), and the rows it would copy against H1's own rows of the same proteins in the root (H1 scanned them in full, so this checks on real data that a copy equals a scan; any difference is reported, not explained away).
Done 2026-11-15 (STATUS row B-47b; `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_b47b_copy_plan_H1.md`): `build-stage` runs a scan as plan, shards and merge whenever another stage on disk measured proteins with the same scan (HMM file sha256, cutoff, Z, tool versions), and the plan copies their rows. On the root, H1's plan copies 5,612 proteins and their 1,787 rows equal H1's own (*measured*). A stage scanned in one process records no plan and is never a source. The places left for card B-47c's inferred rows are `domains._measured_uids` and `domains._measured_rows`.

### A-09 The pod scripts reach wilkcomp02 through a jump host
**L** · found 2026-11-20 by the orchestrator's audit of the 2026-11-17 to 20 campaign
Files: `scripts/pod_pytest.sh`, `scripts/orchestrator/watch_until.sh`, `scripts/orchestrator/watch_ladder.sh` (their `ssh` and `rsync` calls only), `tests/r1/test_pod_pytest_jump.py` (new), `RUNBOOK.md` section 8 (one paragraph)
Why: since the maintenance of 2026-11-17 the pod refuses SSH on port 22 from off campus (`ssh: connect to host wilkcomp02.ccbb.utexas.edu port 22: Connection refused`, *measured*, the PI's laptop, 2026-11-20) and is reached through `wilkcomp03` (`ssh -J if2453@wilkcomp03.ccbb.utexas.edu if2453@wilkcomp02.ccbb.utexas.edu`). `pod_pytest.sh` and the two watch scripts call `ssh` and `rsync -e ssh` with the host alone, so from off campus they fail, and sessions fall back to hand-typed `rsync` and `ssh` lines that the scripts were written to replace (AGENTS §5.5 names `pod_pytest.sh` as the way to run tests).
Do, test first: an optional environment variable `BIOBOX_POD_JUMP` (one `user@host`), passed as `-J` to every `ssh` call and inside the `rsync -e` command of the three scripts. Unset or empty, every command line is what it is today. The test puts stub `ssh` and `rsync` executables first on `PATH` that record their argument lists and exit 0 (no network, no pod): with the variable set every recorded call carries `-J <value>`; unset, none does; a value holding whitespace or a shell metacharacter is refused with exit 2 before any call. RUNBOOK section 8 says when to set it.

**L** · Depends: C-47 (its fix is the model) · found 2026-11-20: card C-47's `check` failed and its queue went on

Why: `c47_driver.sh check` exited 0 after its check script had exited 1, because the script ends `case "$MODE" in ... esac` followed by a `log` line, whose status became the script's; the queue behind it ran `backup` and wrote "all passed", and a waiter started a release on that line (STATUS "6m"). Then: every mode's status is the script's exit status; each mode writes `<mode>/DONE` only on success and refuses to run without the markers of the modes before it; anything that waits on another script tests the marker, never a log line or an exit status alone. No change to what any mode does on success.
Accept: the tests fail first on each driver that has the fault and pass after; `tests/r0` green; the drivers' success paths byte-identical in what they launch (show it with the rehearsal's dry outputs or a `--dry-run`).

### A-15 The release train resumes its own stopped release
**M** · Pod: yes (a rehearsal in scratch; nothing in the data root) · Depends: A-13, A-14 · found 2026-11-21 when DEC-61 allowed `r26.11.2` to resume
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh`, `tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` or `tests/r1/test_c46_release_train.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` ("After a guard stop")
Why: the train's stop message says "rerun to resume", and a rerun of `release_train.sh run` does plan the same release again while it has no `release_def` (the same name, the same stages, the same `build-stage` command, whose published steps are skipped). But the rerun would (1) list the root again into `$WORK/before/listing.tsv`, so that the files the first launch published are no longer "added" and the release's durable copy would miss them; (2) verify the earlier releases again into the same logs, replacing the hashes taken before anything was written; (3) compute `estimate/before.json` again on a root that now holds half the release; (4) launch a unit of the same name, `<prefix>-rel-1`, whose log already holds `pod_run: <unit> exited 143`: card A-14's watch reads a unit's own exit line first, so it would take the new unit for ended at once, fail on "exited 143", and leave the unit building unguarded.
Do, test first with the stubs: `run` recognises a resume (this `$WORK` has a `plan.json` whose release and stages equal the new plan's, and the root has run records of that release and no manifest for it) and then: keeps `before/listing.tsv`, the `before/verify-*.log` files and `estimate/before.json` of the first launch untouched (and refuses if any is missing); still verifies every earlier release now, into a directory of its own, and fails unless each manifest hash equals the first launch's; numbers the unit `<prefix>-rel-<n>` from the units already recorded under `$WORK/run/`, never reusing a name; judges the rate guard against the first launch's estimate. A `$WORK` whose `plan.json` names another release or other stages, with records of an unfinished release in the root, is refused with both named. A first launch is unchanged (the stub command lines' hash). `--dry-run` says `Action: resume <release> (<k> run records published; unit <n>)` in that state. The pack and example waits get the same unit-ended rule as the watch (card A-14 left them).
Accept: the tests fail first on each of the four faults and pass; in scratch on the fixture (`release_train.sh rehearse`'s form), a release is stopped by a `STOP` or by a stubbed guard mid-way and then resumed by `run` alone, and its `root_added.tsv` equals that of an uninterrupted run of the same release (same files, sizes and sha256); `tests/r0` green.

### A-12 One stage driver for every stage
**M** · Pod: yes (the rehearsal on the fixture, in scratch; nothing in the data root) · Depends: A-11 · found 2026-11-21 by card A-11: the same faults were in nine drivers copied from one another, and five more stages are queued (`V2`, `GV1`, `PL2`, `G1`, `SPX1`)
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_driver.sh`, `stage_queue.sh`, `stage_est.py`, `stage_check.py` (new, made from `c47_driver.sh`, `c47_queue.sh`, `c47_est.py`, `c47_check.py` and `c48c_v1_*`), `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stages/<STAGE>.conf` (new: one small file per queued stage), `scripts/orchestrator/rel_wait.sh` only for the unit patterns it lists, `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py` (new), `RUNBOOK.md` (a section "Building a stage" and its Appendix A rows)
Why: each stage so far had a driver written for it by copying the last one, with its stage name, input sums, releases and backup name written into the script. Card A-11 fixed one fault in nine copies. A stage's build is the same sequence every time; what differs is a short list of values.
Do, test first with stub commands on `PATH` as `tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` does: one driver that takes `STAGE` and reads `stages/<STAGE>.conf` (the inputs directory and the sha256 of `genomes.json` and `objects.json`; depth; `--min-contig-len` if any; the library arguments or none; `--crispr` or not; the stages a later stage must wait for; estimates the stage's note recorded, where the code's estimate is to be judged against another figure, as `GV1`'s gene calling against 236 CPU-minutes; accepted records, each with its decision row, as `c47_est.py`'s `ACCEPTED`). Modes, each exiting with its own status, writing `<mode>/DONE` only on success and refusing to run without the markers before it: `estimate` (from the code, never a copied number), `rehearse` (the fixture, in scratch), `before` (the root's listing, `release verify` of every release the root defines, read from the root and not from a list in the script), `run` (`build-stage` through `pod_run.sh` in the image by digest, the `STOP` check, the guards of DEC-43 and DEC-53, 280 CPU-minutes per process, free space), `after`, `check` (what `c47_check.py` asserts, with the within-stage ledger rows read as card C-47 corrected them; the list of files added with sha256), `backup` (the tar on `/stor/work` under a temporary name, read back, `mv -n`; never overwrites). `stage_queue.sh` runs them in order on the markers. Unit names are `<stage>-*` in lower case, and `rel_wait.sh` takes the pattern from its argument. The thread cap comes from the profile and the hour, as `build-stage`'s allowance does; nothing in the driver names a thread count above the profile's.
The values of the queued stages come from their notes and are written into their `.conf` files with the note named: `V2` and `GV1` (`planning/measurements/2026-11-20_virus_v2_gv1_inputs.md`, `2026-11-20_virus_gv1_inputs.md`; `GV1` after `V2`), `PL2` (card C-40's note), `G1` (card C-41's), `SPX1` (`2026-11-20_spire_tranche_1.md`: `--depth called --min-contig-len 5000`, after `G1`). A stage whose note leaves a value open gets no `.conf`; name it in the report.
Accept: the tests (each mode's inner command failing in turn: non-zero exit, no marker; the success path's command lines recorded); the rehearsal on the fixture builds a stage with the new driver and its `check` passes; `c47_driver.sh`'s and the new driver's `run` launch the same `build-stage` command for `C5`'s values apart from the unit name (shown from the stubs' records). No stage is built in the data root by this card: the first real use is `V2`, the orchestrator's, after release `r26.11.2` is audited.
**Amended 2026-11-21 after an independent review of the first hand-back (`task/A-12` at `d772cb7`; an Opus reviewer read the code and ran nothing; it found no path by which a mode exits 0 or writes its marker after a failure, and these defects). Not merged until each is fixed, test first, or answered with evidence.** Files added for this: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh` (only the list of units its own lock looks for).
(1) **`run` can abandon a build that is still running.** Its loop ends on any non-zero exit of `systemctl --user is-active`, a bus error included; the driver then writes `run/FAILED` and exits while the unit builds on in the data root with no guard. Leave the loop only on the unit's own exit line or an explicit inactive or not-found state; otherwise keep polling. `stop_unit` must see the unit gone before it says so. A trap stops the unit if the driver itself exits during `run`.
(2) **A release can start while the next stage builds.** `rel_wait.sh` looks for active units once, before a wait of hours, and `release_train.sh`'s own lock knows no stage unit. Both take the unit patterns of every `stages/*.conf` (and the older names), and `rel_wait.sh` looks again immediately before the train starts.
(3) **`stage_check.py` and `stage_est.py rates` and `complete` are run by no test** (every driver test stubs them). On a constructed root, each assertion of the check is seen to fail once (a ledger count that differs from the contigs held, an uncovered contig, gene rows for a contig not stored, copied domain rows that differ from their source, a CRISPR count that differs, a record of a step the stage's depth does not have, a contig under the least length) and to pass; `rates` runs on a root with a published gene-call plan, scan shards, a `JUDGE` line and an `ACCEPTED` record.
(4) **A marker is not tied to what it was made for.** Each `DONE` records the sha256 of the conf; a mode refuses a marker made for another conf, the rehearsal's included; `run` checks the inputs' sums again.
(5) **The unit's quota is the night cap at any hour.** The kernel quota is the only hard limit on the account's threads. The driver sets the unit's `CPUQuota` at launch, and again at each poll when the hour's cap changes, to the profile's cap of that hour less what the driver's own helpers may use, so that the unit and the helpers together stay inside the cap by day and by night.
(6) **Checks the model drivers made against their notes.** A conf line `EXPECT=held_by <STAGE> <n> <note>`: `estimate` stops when the contigs an earlier stage holds differ from the note's (for `GV1`: `V2` holds 17,664). The check keeps each run's report (a numbered directory, as card C-47's `CHECK_DIR` did). The check reads the ledger's reason strings as `c47_check.py` corrected them, no looser, and uses every index row where `c47_check.py` did.
(7) **Silent passes in the check**: a missing scan plan when the conf has a library fails; CRISPR paths that match nothing when the conf has `CRISPR=yes` fail unless the ledger says no arrays were found; a judged-size step that `rates` could not name fails.
(8) **The environment.** The root is compared after `realpath` (a trailing slash made the data root pass as a scratch root, with the rehearsal gate skipped); in the data root the conf must be the tree's own `stages/<STAGE>.conf`, `MEM` is at most 200G, the free-space floors cannot be lowered, and an `ACCEPTED` or `JUDGE` line's decision row or note must exist (`| DEC-n |` in `planning/DECISIONS.md`; the note's file). A record stopped against a `JUDGE` figure can be accepted by a decision row as any other record can, by name and value, without deleting the `JUDGE` line.
(9) Smaller: the backup compares two hashes that must each be 64 hex characters; a `STOP` during `run` still judges the records published before it exits; each rehearsal starts with its own logs; `rates` takes from `estimate/before.json` what does not change during a run and its time per call is reported for the fixture; the queue reads `run/FAILED` before it re-runs `estimate` and `before`.
**Amended again 2026-11-21 after the second review (`4996467`: the first round's defects closed, each with a test).** Six further points went to the agent (the release side read its confs from the suite's frozen tree; the watch had no time limit; a stop during a long helper call; a rehearsal-only conf accepted in the data root; `PROFILE` free in the data root; assertions of the check without a test) and are done at `2f0cc64` but for one, which needs this line: **`tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` joins the card's Files for a migration of exactly two tests and one helper.** `rel_wait.sh` is to refuse a tree without `stages/*.conf` and to take the suite as a required argument (no default `rel1120`); `test_rel_wait_starts_the_release_when_every_marker_is_there` calls it with no argument, and `test_rel_wait_takes_the_driver_directory_and_the_suite_as_arguments` gives it a tree without `stages/`. Migrate both: the suite is passed, the tree has a `stages/` with one conf; what each asserts about markers, the dry run and the run is unchanged, and no other test of the file is touched. Add beside them a test that the bare call and the tree without confs are refused. ### A-10 The OOM-killed search's recorded peak can miss its bound on GitHub Actions
**M** · Depends: none · found 2026-11-20 on a commit that changed only documents (`b17935e`, Actions run 44761392180)
Files: `src/biobox/search/runner.py` (the peak it records for a worker the kernel killed), `src/biobox/runstep.py` only if the sampling helper is shared, `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py` (the test below; no bound is widened), a note in `planning/measurements/`
Why: `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py::test_c16_an_oom_killed_search_records_a_peak_within_20_percent_of_its_cap` failed with `recorded 596.7 MiB (scope_memory_peak_sampled); cap 953.7 MiB; status oom`, so `0.8 * cap <= peak` did not hold (*measured*, Actions, 2026-11-20; 1 failed, 2,501 passed, 252 skipped). The same test passed on the two pushes before it (`352424f`, `becdc52`) and the commit touched no code, so the outcome depends on timing: the recorded value is a sample of the scope's `memory.peak` taken while the worker ran, and the kernel killed the worker between two samples, after the allocation had grown past the last one. The sibling test for a step (`test_c16_an_oom_killed_step_records_a_peak_within_20_percent_of_its_cap`) read 256.0 MiB against a cap of 256.0 MiB in the same run, with source `scope_memory_peak` (read after the end, not sampled). A record that can be 37% under the true peak is also a wrong number in a run record (AGENTS §6), apart from the test.
Do, test first: find out why the search worker's scope gives a sampled value where the step's gives the final one (the scope collected before it is read, a different unit type, the order of kill and read), and make the search runner record the final `memory.peak` of the worker's scope when the kernel killed it, as `runstep` does; keep the sampled value only as the stated fallback, with its source named. Reproduce the miss deterministically first (a fault that allocates in one step between two samples, or a sampling interval the test sets long), see the bound fail, then pass. Do not widen the 20% bound, add a retry or skip the test.
Accept: the reproduction fails before and passes after; `scripts/orchestrator/ci_repeat.sh` shows the test green on 10 consecutive Actions runs of the branch.
**Diagnosed 2026-11-20, not fixed: the card's "Why" is half wrong and its "Do" cannot be done inside its files** (an Opus agent read the code and 31 Actions logs and stopped before any change, AGENTS §9; its reading, with what is measured marked). (1) The search runner and `run-step` use the same helper, placed the same way inside the scope (`runstep.measured`, `runstep.step_peak`), and both normally read the final `memory.peak` after the worker is reaped: on Actions the search's OOM test recorded `953.7 MiB (scope_memory_peak)`, exactly the cap, in 30 of 31 job attempts, and the step's test `256.0 MiB (scope_memory_peak)` in 31 of 31 (*measured*, `gh run view --log` of runs 44514423589 to 44775879527, read 2026-11-20). The sampled value is a fallback used only when **the helper itself** dies before writing its final file; the sampling interval is not the cause. (2) In the failing attempt the helper was killed at about the moment of the worker's OOM kill (status `oom`, no final file, no sample after 596.7 MiB although it samples every 0.2 s). No path in the runner sends that kill. Who did is not settled by the code or the log: the kernel's memory-cgroup OOM killer taking the helper as a second victim, systemd stopping the scope (`OOMPolicy=stop`), or `systemd-oomd`; the worker's `stderr.txt` is deleted with its work directory. (3) No ordering inside the helper fixes it: the final value needs a survivor reading `memory.peak` while the scope's cgroup still exists, and the cgroup goes when its last live process does. Ten green runs would not show a fix either: at 1 failure in 31, ten runs pass by chance about 72% of the time (*derived*: (30/31)^10). (4) **Exposure is small**: a run record under the root's `runs/` is published only on exit 0, which implies the final file was written; the pod's root has 0 sampled sources in 1,908 records (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20). A sampled source can reach only the record of a failed step in staging and `search_run` rows of runs that ended `oom`, `timeout` or killed. (5) Design choices, for a decision (STATUS open item 20): (a) a keeper process in the scope that allocates nothing, or the helper moved outside the scope; (b) the peak from systemd's own accounting of a named unit (`MemoryPeak`; whether it outlives the unit on the Actions image is not verified); (c) a sampler that charges nothing to the scope (one open file read with `pread`, a pre-faulted output file), which helps only if the second-victim reading is right. Card A-10a gathers the evidence that chooses between them.

### A-10b A workflow that repeats only the memory-peak tests
**L** · Depends: A-10a · the Actions part of cards A-10 and A-10a without running the whole suite sixty times
Files: `.github/workflows/peak-evidence.yml` (new), `tests/r0/test_runbook.py` or the r0 test that lists the workflows only if one must learn the new file's name (say which), `RUNBOOK.md` section 8 (three lines)
Why: the sampled case appeared in 1 of 31 `ci` jobs, and a `ci` job is the whole suite, about 55 minutes of a hosted runner (*measured*, Actions run 44761392180: 3,355 s of pytest). Sixty of those to see it once more is about 55 runner-hours; the four C-16 tests alone take about a minute.
Do: a workflow with `workflow_dispatch` only (no `push`, no `pull_request`, no schedule), input `repeats` (default 60, at most 200), one job on `ubuntu-24.04` set up as `ci.yml` is (same actions at the same versions, `uv sync --python 3.14`). It runs `uv run pytest -q tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py tests/r2/test_runstep.py -k c16 -rA -W always` `repeats` times in a shell loop that **does not stop at a failure**, appends each run's output to a log, and at the end prints, for every iteration, the `C16PeakEvidence` lines (they hold `peak_evidence`), the count of iterations whose OOM tests recorded a sampled source, and the count that failed; the job **fails if any iteration failed** (nothing is hidden, nothing is retried: every iteration's result is reported). The log is uploaded as an artifact. ### C-28 The night thread allowance mistakes our own load for someone else's
**M** · found 2026-11-10 (`phase2/FAILED.2315-refused-48`) and again 2026-11-12 · Depends: C-12
Files: `src/biobox/runstep.py` or wherever the hour's allowance is computed (`scan_allowance`), `src/biobox/stage.py` if it calls it, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/r50_driver.sh` (`NIGHT_JOBS`), `RUNBOOK.md`, tests
Why: AGENTS §7.2 allows 56 threads at night "only when `ps` or `top` shows no other compute". The code tests the load average instead (above 8 means the day cap, 40), and our own scan holds the load near 42, so a build can never use more than 40 threads at night: on 2026-11-12 at 23:24 CST the account used 41.1 CPUs, every other user together under 0.5 (*measured*, `ps`), on a 112-core host. A scan at 13 shards of 4 threads instead of 10 would be 30% faster at night (*derived*).
Do, test first: the night allowance asks what the rule asks: the CPU other users are using now (summed `pcpu` of processes not owned by the account, over a short window, with a stated threshold such as one core), not the load average; the day cap is unchanged; the total stays a cap over everything the account runs (AGENTS §7.2 and the memory "pod thread cap is a total"): the allowance of a step is the cap minus what the account's other processes use, so tests and agents are not squeezed out; say how a step that started under 56 behaves when the day window opens (it must not run on at 56: the existing re-read before each launch, card C-12). A test with injected readings for each case (others busy, others idle, own load high). The driver's night shard count follows the allowance instead of a constant. Never applied to a build's tree during a scan.
Accept: the tests; on the pod, `run-step` reports the allowance it computed and why.

### B-48 Gene calling in batch steps under the CPU budget
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only on the data root; scratch builds) · Depends: B-30 (its form), C-20 · blocks card C-26 part 2 (stage `PL1`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (the `stage genecall` actions), tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, `RUNBOOK.md` (§2 and the limits row), one measurement note
Why: gene calling is one step and one process, with `--workers` threads. It cost 143 to 154 CPU-minutes on each stage built so far (S1, P2, P3, H1; *measured*, their run records), under the 300 of AGENTS §7.2 by luck of their sizes: nothing estimates it before launch, and nothing splits it. The PLSDB stage `PL1` is estimated at about 860 CPU-minutes (*derived*: 7,102 Mbp at 7.26 s per Mbp; the rate *measured* on every 30th record, `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_plsdb_inputs.md`: circular genomes in meta mode cost 7.70 s per Mbp with their wrap flanks, linear ones 2.94, where the prokaryote stages ran at 0.93, *derived* from P3's record). So `PL1` cannot be built as the code is.
Do, test first: (1) an estimate of the step before it starts, from the stage's inputs (bases by kind, topology and the gene-calling mode the code will choose for each genome), at rates measured from the existing stages' run records and the `PL1` sample (state each rate with its source; a test holds each estimate within 20% above its record, as card W-35's does); a refusal (exit 2) of a one-process call over `SHARD_MARGIN` of the budget when batches are not possible, else the split, decided by `build-stage` as it decides ingest's (card B-30) and the scans'. (2) Gene calling in batch steps: a plan (genomes in a fixed order, cut by estimated cost so that each batch fits the margin), one `run-step` process per batch, up to `--scan-jobs` at once within the allowance of the hour, `STOP` checked between launches, each batch publishing its own part under a temporary name; then a merge under the step name `genecall` that writes exactly the files, ledger and run record's result of a one-process call. A batch with output is not rerun; a killed batch leaves nothing a reader takes. (3) **Byte identity with the one-process call**: on the fixture at 1, 2, 3 and 5 batches and at 1 and 3 jobs; and on the pod, in scratch (a view of the root, card C-33's `c33_view.py`), stage S1 or H1 gene-called again in batches and compared with the root's own gene-call files by sha256 (every table gene calling writes, and the ledger; the root is only read). If byte identity cannot hold for a table (for example a training step that sees the whole stage), stop and report what depends on the whole stage: do not change what gene calling outputs. (4) Bounded memory per batch (the memory test of AGENTS §4.2); determinism at any batch count, job count and thread count. (5) Optional, only if the bytes do not change: a genome all of whose contigs another stage owns (DEC-26) is not called at all (on `PL1` about 143 of the 860 CPU-minutes re-call 7,046 such genomes and drop the result, *derived*); say what the ledger records for it today and keep that.
Accept: the tests; the byte identity on the fixture and on a real stage in scratch; the estimate for `PL1` printed by `build-stage --dry-run` or its equivalent on the real inputs (`/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/PL1/`), with the batch plan and each batch under the margin. The build of `PL1` itself is card C-26 part 2.

Left: a batch is not a `run-step` step, so the root records only the merge's cost: card B-50.

### B-50 A run record for each gene-calling batch
**M** · Depends: B-48 · before card C-26 part 2 (the first stage built in batches)
Files: `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` (one artifact for what a batch stages, or whatever `run-step` needs to accept a batch step: say exactly what), `tests/registry/schema_lock.json` and `tests/registry/json_schema_lock.json` only by running `scripts/update_schema_lock.py` (this card says so), `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (the `stage genecall` actions), tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, `RUNBOOK.md` (§2, §5 and the limits row)
Why: since card B-48 a stage over the gene-calling budget is called in batches, each a process of its own, and only the merge is a `run-step`. So the root's run record `runs/stage=<S>/genecall.json` of such a stage holds the merge's cost (0.43 CPU-minutes on S1 in scratch, *measured*) and nothing in the root says what each batch cost; the batches' CPU is in the build's log and in scratch, which is cleared. Every other step that runs in pieces records each piece (`ingest.batch-<j>`, `domains-<lib>.shard-<k>`, the clustering parts), the drivers' rate guards and the 300 CPU-minute check read run records, and the gene-calling rates of card B-48 were fitted from them.
Do, test first: each batch runs through `run-step` as a step `genecall.batch-<j>` of the stage, with a run record in `runs/stage=<S>/` like an ingest batch's (its CPU-minutes, wall time, peak memory, image and code identity, its estimate), and the plan as `genecall.plan`; what a batch stages is published where `run-step` requires (state where; a batch's rows are intermediate: say whether they are an artifact of the root that the merge then supersedes, as ingest's batch outputs are, or stay in scratch with only the record published, and why that satisfies AGENTS §3.9 and §3.10). The merge, the bytes of every gene-call table and the ledger, and the one-process path do not change: card B-48's byte-identity tests pass unchanged, and a new test compares a stage called in batches before and after this card. `release verify` and the manifest treat the new records as they treat ingest's. A stage with batch records but no merge record is resumed, not restarted. Regenerate the schema locks with the script if the registry changes, and say what changed in them.
Accept: the tests; on the fixture, `runs/stage=<S>/` of a stage called in 3 batches holds `genecall.plan.json`, three `genecall.batch-<j>.json` and `genecall.json`, and their CPU-minutes sum to the step's cost; the manifest hash of the fixture as GitHub Actions computes it is reported (it changes if the registry does: say so, so the orchestrator can update the peer hash on the pod).
Amended 2026-11-15 after the agent stopped before any edit, rightly: the card said card B-48's tests "pass unchanged", and five of their assertions state B-48's design, which this card reverses. **Design, decided here**: a batch's rows stay in scratch; one new artifact `genecall_plan` at `l1/genecall_plan/stage=<S>/(plan|batch-<j>).json`, not in the manifest and `small_ok`, as `ingest_plan` is; the steps `genecall.plan` and `genecall.batch-<j>` each stage one small JSON (the plan; the batch's `done.json`), which `run-step` publishes with a run record `runs/stage=<S>/genecall.<step>.json`. **Migration of `tests/r2/test_b48_genecall_batches.py`, and only this**: (1) the assertion that `runs/stage=S1/` of a batched build holds only `genecall.json`, `ingest.json` and `inputs.json` (and its docstring's "nothing in `runs/` names a batch") becomes the new listing: those three, `genecall.plan.json` and one `genecall.batch-<j>.json` per batch; (2) the four comparisons of the whole root tree with the one-process build leave out `l1/genecall_plan/`, as `tests/r2/test_ingest_batches.py` leaves out `l0seq/ingest_plan/`; every other path is still compared, and the direct comparisons of the gene-call files (`genecall_files(...) ==`) are not touched. List each migrated assertion with what it asserted before and after. A batch whose record is published and whose scratch rows are gone is redone and checked against the record's counts and genome digest (the scratch rows' order depends on the workers, so not by sha256): test it.
Done 2026-11-15 (STATUS row B-50): the plan and each batch are `run-step` steps with records in `runs/stage=<S>/` and `l1/genecall_plan/stage=<S>/`; the rows stay in scratch; the gene-call files, the ledger and the one-process path are byte for byte as before; the schema locks and the fixture's manifest hash did not change. The plan record is per stage, not per gene-call version: a second gene-call version of a batched stage is refused at the published plan.

### B-49 A release takes an earlier release's clustering when its proteins are the same (DEC-45 (2))
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only on the data root; scratch views) · Depends: B-41, B-45, C-19 · DEC-45 (PI, 2026-11-15) · needed before ncRNA on the built stages (DEC-42) and any release that adds a producer and no protein
Files: `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py` (or the module that plans and writes the cluster tables), `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (the `release cluster` actions), `src/biobox/l3/tokens.py` (the refusal below only), `src/biobox/catalog.py` only if `verify_manifest` or the manifest must know the new ledger step, tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, `RUNBOOK.md` (§2 and the limits row), a measurement note
Why: a release reads only its own `release=<R>` cluster files, so a new release over stages whose proteins did not change still clusters them all again: about 14,087 CPU-minutes for the 45,815,843 proteins of `rM.2` (*derived* from its run records, card C-33). The bytes would be the ones we have: `rM.1` and `rM.2`, two separate clustering runs of the same proteins at different thread counts, gave byte-identical `cluster_member`, `cluster_member_aln` and `member_cluster` files, and cluster plans equal but for the release's name (*measured*, 2026-11-14, STATUS handoff).
Do, test first: (1) State what "the same proteins" means in terms the code already records: the cluster plan's input hashes (and the tool versions and parameters in the plan), computed for the new release before any clustering; say exactly which fields must be equal and which may differ (the release's name; the plan hash that covers it). (2) `release cluster` (and so `build-stage`) for a release R2, when an earlier release R1 of the same root has a finished clustering whose plan inputs equal R2's: no clustering call is run; R2's `release=R2` cluster files are written as copies of R1's (new files; R1's are never touched; no links across releases, since a release must verify by its own files) with R2's own cluster plan records, a ledger row and a run record that name R1 as the source and both plans' input hashes; when several earlier releases qualify, the choice is deterministic and stated. `--no-reuse` forces a clustering. (3) Byte identity: on the fixture, R2 built by reuse equals R2 built by clustering, file for file by sha256, for every table of the release (cluster tables, tokens, postings, dimension tables, manifest but for fields that name the run), and `release verify` passes on both; when one protein differs (a stage added, or a sequence changed) nothing is reused and the test shows the clustering ran. (4) A release R2 over a superset of R1's stages reuses nothing in this card (partial reuse is a later question: say what it would need). (5) Separately, found by card C-33: the tokens step builds tokens with no cluster levels, without an error, when its release has no `cluster_member` files; it must refuse (exit 2) naming the missing clustering; see it build silently first. (6) On the pod, in a scratch view of the root (links; card C-33's `c33_view.py`): define a scratch release over the seven stages of `rM.2`, run its cluster step, and show that it reuses `rM.2`'s (or `rM.1`'s) clustering in seconds or minutes, with the copied files' sha256 equal to the source's and the release's later steps (tokens, postings, dimension tables, manifest, verify) running on top; report the CPU-minutes against the 14,087 of a clustering. Nothing is written in the real root.
Accept: the tests; the scratch release on the pod verified, with its cost; the tokens refusal.
Done 2026-11-15 but for item (5) (STATUS row B-49; `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_cluster_reuse.md`): a release whose plan equals an earlier release's but for the name takes its clustering as copies (`release cluster reuse`; `build-stage` does it by default, `--no-reuse` clusters); the scratch release `rB49.1` took `rM.2`'s in 3.29 CPU-minutes against 14,086.8 (*measured*). The plan's cut is part of what must be equal, so a release over `rM.2`'s stages reuses its clustering only when built with `--cluster-parts 24`. Item (5), the tokens refusal, is card B-51: it needs a migration of two tests that this card did not allow.

New tests beside the three written: with the option, a release without a clustering builds and its result reports no cluster-level tokens; with the option and a clustering present, exit 2 and nothing written.
Done 2026-11-16 (STATUS row B-51): `release tokens` refuses a release with protein slots and no clustering; `--without-clusters` is the one way out and is itself refused when a clustering is present; four test files pass it where MMseqs2 is absent.

### B-55 Buffer `crispr._Scan` scratch Parquet writes to `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` before multi-million-contig stages
**M** · Pod: yes (tests, and byte-identity check against one built stage in scratch; read-only on the data root) · Depends: E-12, E-15 · blocks `--crispr` on `PL2` (`C-48c`), `G1` (`C-41`) and unbinned metagenomes (`C-42`, `C-48e`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/crispr.py`, tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-19, card C-42 `SP0` pilot): on `3,930,592` unfiltered SPIRE contigs (`3.226 Gbp`), MinCED finished in ~8 minutes (`437` arrays, `6,743` elements), after which `crispr._Scan` (`contig()`, `skip()`, and `_collect()`) left `3,930,592` 1-row Parquet row groups in scratch `contigs.parquet` (`2,188,804,196` bytes = `2.19 GB` of Parquet footer metadata, *measured* via `pyarrow.parquet.ParquetFile`) because `pq.ParquetWriter.write_batch` without `row_group_size` starts a new row group on every 1-row `RecordBatch`. When `crispr._publish` queried `contigs` twice in DuckDB (`memory_limit = 256MB`), parsing `3.93M` row-group footers grew process RSS to `29.90 GB` (`31,354,484 kB`, *measured*, `ps`) and burned `>105` CPU-minutes on metadata alone (AGENTS §4.1, §4.6). Stages `PL2` (`859,036` new contigs, *measured*, card C-40) and `G1` (`1,227,222` contigs, *measured*, card C-41) would hit the same bottleneck under `--crispr`.
Do, test first: (1) Reproduce first in a test: scan `10,000` synthetic contigs (with stubbed `_search` in `tests/r1` and with real MinCED on a smaller multi-contig batch in `tests/r2`) and assert that every scratch Parquet file (`contigs.parquet`, `arrays.parquet`, `elements.parquet`, `topology.parquet`) has at most `max(1, ceil(num_rows / io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS))` row groups (`io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS = 122_880`, matching `_TOPO_ROWS`), plus the 100× synthetic store memory test of AGENTS §4.2 (peak RSS growth stated and bounded). See the row-group assertion fail first on today's code (`>= 10,000` row groups). (2) Buffer scratch rows in `_Scan` for `contigs`, `arrays` and `elements` (flushing in chunks of `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` and flushing the remainder in `close(ok=True)`, passing `row_group_size=io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS`), keeping bounded memory (at most one `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` batch per scratch table in Python). (3) Byte identity: published `feature`, `l1_crispr` and `ledger` files of both `crispr.run` and `crispr.run_near_cas` are byte-identical to today's on the `micro_s0` fixture (shared-contig and circular-origin cases included) and, on `wilkcomp02` in scratch (read-only on `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`), on stage `S1` or `H1` compared by `sha256` against the root's published CRISPR files.
Accept: the row-group and 100× RSS tests fail first and pass after; byte-identity on the fixture and against `S1` or `H1` on the pod; fixture `manifest_hash` unchanged.
Done 2026-11-19 (STATUS row B-55): `crispr._ScratchWriter` buffers `contigs`, `arrays`, `elements` and `topology` scratch rows up to `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` (`122,880`) and flushes full chunks via `write_batch(..., row_group_size=io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS)` (`6 passed` on new R1/R2 row-group & 100× RSS tests, `4/4` `H1` published CRISPR files sha256-identical, full pod suite `2,549 passed, 24 skipped`, *measured*).

### B-56 Avoid `KeyLookup` 64-row-group LRU thrashing above 7.86 M contigs in `genecall`
**L** · Depends: B-48, B-50 · uncovered 2026-11-19 by card C-42 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-18_spire_pilot.md` §4.4 #2) before multi-million-contig real-root stages (`PL2` + `G1..G41` + `S5` + `SPIRE`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `src/biobox/l0seq/lookup.py` (only if needed), tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-19, card C-42): `genecall` iterates `genome_input` ordered by `genome_uid = blake3(...)`, which is pseudorandom relative to `seq_locator`'s `raw_digest` sort key. Once cumulative stored contigs in a root exceed `64 × 122,880 = 7,864,320` rows (`> 64` row groups; observed when `SPN1` `4.38M` + `SPQ1` `4.99M` reached `9.37M` = `78` row groups), random `genome_uid` order misses `KeyLookup`'s 64-row-group LRU cache on `(78 - 64)/78 = 18%` of lookups, decompressing a `122,880`-row Arrow row group per miss (`>3×` CPU inflation and `>100 GB` aggregate RSS across workers).
Do, test first: order 1-contig genomes inside `genecall` work/batch execution by `raw_digest` / `seq_locator` order (while emitting identical sorted output tables and deterministic batch digests, or migrating only the plan hash if batch assignment changes — keep single-stage byte-identity on `micro_s0` and `S1`), or replace `KeyLookup`'s full-row-group cache in `genecall` with a bounded-memory streaming/sorted join; verify 100× RSS test and O(1) row-group reads above `64` row groups.
Accept: reproduction test with `> 64` `seq_locator` row groups fails/thrashes before and reads each row group O(1) times after; published gene-call outputs byte-identical.
**Not startable as written (2026-11-20; a Sonnet agent read the code and stopped before editing, AGENTS §9; its reading, not yet checked against a profile).** Gene calling resolves keys on two unrelated sort orders: `SeqStore.get` looks `contig_uid` up in `contig_index` (sorted by `contig_uid, raw_digest`) and then `raw_digest` in `seq_locator` (sorted by `raw_digest`); `genecall._genome_bases` and `ContigOwners.owner` are two more `contig_uid` lookups, each a `KeyLookup` with the same 64-row-group cache. Ordering genomes by `raw_digest`, as "Do" says, makes only the `seq_locator` lookup sequential; the three `contig_index` lookups stay random, so "reads each row group O(1) times" cannot be met inside `genecall.py` and `lookup.py`. A per-batch sorted prefetch inside the card's files gives about 2× (*derived* by the agent from the note's 18% miss rate: about 156 against 324 row-group reads per 600-contig batch), not O(1). A real fix is a sorted merge join of `contig_uid` → `raw_digest` → locator for the stage, which changes the order the gene-call plan and batch digests are defined on (card B-50) and touches `src/biobox/l0seq/inputs.py` and `store.py`. **This needs a design decision (tier H) before a card: STATUS open item 16.** It does not block any stage on the pod today: the root holds about 0.13 M contigs against the threshold of 7.86 M (*derived*: `r26.11.1`'s 122,626 contigs plus `C5`'s). **Corrected the same day**: `PL2` does not cross it. With `V1` (168,358 new contigs), `C1` to `C6` (16,470), `PL2` (859,036) and `G1` (1,227,222) the root holds about 2.4 M contigs (*derived*: the sum with 122,626; each count *measured* in its card's note); the stages that cross 7.86 M are the IMG-UMAG prokaryote MAGs (`J2` to `J15`, about 79,000 contigs per Gbp in card C-48d's pilot, *measured*) and GlobDB's `G2` on. The card's header names `PL2` among "multi-million-contig" stages; the threshold is cumulative, and `PL2` alone is 0.86 M.

**L** · Depends: B-52, C-47 · uncovered 2026-11-19 by card C-47 (`C5.ingest`: `45.803` vs `38.057` CPU-min, `1.204×`)

Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-19, card C-47): all 9 stages `B-52` fitted on (`S1`, `P1..P6`, `H1`, `PL1`) ingested uncompressed `.fna` files, whereas `C5` reads `2,032` `.fna.gz` archives (`2.78 GB` `.gz` -> `9.51 GB` FASTA), adding `~0.84` CPU-min per uncompressed GB (`~7.7` CPU-min) of zlib decompression (`45.803` measured vs `38.057` estimated = `1.2035×`).

Accept: tests fail first on `C5` (`> 20%`) and pass within `15%` across all 10 built/ingested stages.
**Amended 2026-11-21: the card's Evidence is wrong, and the card is now a measurement before any term is written** (a Sonnet agent measured before editing and stopped, AGENTS §9; *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-21, one process in the test image at `nice 19`, CPU time from `getrusage`, with the pod's load average near 54). Reading gzip FASTA costs far less than the card says: Python's `gzip` on 6 of `SPX1`'s files, 0.0901 CPU-min per GB of FASTA (0.079 to 0.097); the ingest reader `biobox.fasta.iter_records` on 3 `SPX1` files, 0.1278 per GB from `.gz` against 0.0449 from a plain copy of the same bytes; on 100 of `C5`'s own `.fna.gz` files, 0.2664 per GB of bases. So decompression explains about 2 of `C5`'s 7.75 CPU-minutes over its estimate (*derived*: 45.803 − 38.057; 0.27 × 9.51 less the parsing already in the rate), not all of it as the card's 0.84 per GB assumed; about 5.6 are unexplained. `V1` read one plain file and ran at 1.021 of its estimate: no second gzip stage exists. The eleven built stages' ratios, record over estimate: `S1` 0.858, `P1` 1.072, `P2` 0.958, `P3` 1.058, `P4` 1.034, `P5` 1.075, `P6` 0.999, `H1` 1.012, `PL1` 1.034, `V1` 1.021, `C5` 1.2035 (*derived* by the agent from the run records and the code's formula).
**Do now, instead of the Do above**: find what `C5`'s ingest spent the rest on, by measurement in a scratch root (never the data root; nothing under `/stor`), before any change to `src/`. Candidates, each with an arm that separates it: (1) gzip against plain: the same genomes ingested from `.fna.gz` and from plain copies; (2) a cost per object: the same bases as many objects and as few; (3) contigs an earlier stage holds: the estimate charges nothing for them (`bytes − held`), but ingest still reads and hashes them, and `C5` has 2,550 such contigs, 267,273,180 bp, where the stages the rate was fitted on had few: ingest a set as one stage, then the same set as a second stage of the same scratch root, where every contig is held; (4) load: CPU time per unit of work rises when the pod's hardware threads are shared; repeat an arm at different loads if the night gives them, and record the load average and what else of ours ran with each arm, and what ran beside `C5`'s ingest on 2026-11-19 from 11:22 to 12:08 against the fitted stages' ingests. Use a sample of `C5`'s own inputs (`/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/C5`, read-only; about 200 genomes, each arm a few CPU-minutes, each arm three times, interleaved), through `build-stage` or the ingest step as `C5`'s driver ran it, in the image. Then write the note, and only if the arms name a cause that explains `C5`'s record to within 15% write the term for that cause, test first as the card's Accept says, with the fitted stages' estimates unchanged to the byte. If no arm explains it, stop with the note: a term fitted to one record with no mechanism is not written.
Files, added for the measurement: one script under `scripts/stress/` and a note in `planning/measurements/`.
**`SPX1` no longer waits on this card for the gzip term** (decompression is about 2% of its ingest estimate, *derived*: 0.09 × 256.9 against 1,056). What `SPX1`'s ingest estimate does need is a look at `--min-contig-len`: the code charges every byte at the stored rate (*derived*: 256.9 GB × 4.11 = 1,056) although 85.23% of the bases are under 5 kb and are read and dropped; report what the arms say about the cost of a base that is read and not stored (arm 3 measures the same thing), because that sets how `SPX1`'s first-pass shards should be cut.

**Measured 2026-11-21, and closed without a term** (STATUS row B-58; `planning/measurements/2026-11-21_c5_ingest_cost.md`, `scripts/stress/b58_ingest_arms.py`). On 204 of `C5`'s own objects, three interleaved runs per arm in scratch roots (*measured*, wilkcomp02): no cost per object (one object against 204: 1.7% less); contigs an earlier stage holds cost 0.38 CPU-min per GB, 0.10 CPU-minutes on `C5`; the earlier stages' index adds nothing; the cost per byte does not grow with the stage; gzip costs 0.16 to 0.26 per GB (two reading passes), 1.6 to 2.5 of `C5`'s 7.75 CPU-minutes; plain copies of `C5`'s genomes already cost 4.39 per GB against the rate's 4.11; runs at a load of 52 to 56 cost 6.7% more than at 20 to 37. A third of `C5` (678 objects) projects the whole stage at 43.1 CPU-minutes, 5.9% under its record. So the record of DEC-56 is the sum of three small things and no one term explains it to within 15%; none is written. **What it found for `SPX1`**: with `--min-contig-len 5000` a base read and dropped costs 0.299 CPU-min per Gbp and a base stored 4.94, so its ingest is about 264 CPU-minutes (*derived*, from 4 files) where the code says 1,056. Card B-61 takes both terms into the estimate.

### B-59 A gene-calling rate for giant-virus genomes
**L** · Depends: B-48 · found 2026-11-20 by card C-48c part 3 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-20_virus_gv1_inputs.md`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py` (the per-kind cost model only), `tests/r1/test_b48_genecall_estimate.py` or a new `tests/r1/test_b59_giant_virus_rate.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` rates
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20, card C-48c part 3): on 695 sampled GVMAGs V2 genomes (174.3 Mbp) gv-meta gene calling took 2.7239 s per Mbp, 35.2% under the code's gv-meta rate of 4.2019 s per Mbp, which was fitted on phage-sized genomes; the code's estimate of stage `GV1`'s gene-calling step (354 CPU-min) is then 48% over what the sample predicts (236 at 2.80 s per Mbp with the step overhead). A driver's rate guard judges a step against the code's estimate (DEC-43, DEC-53: more than 20% under is recorded, not stopped, so `GV1` would not stop on it), and batch plans built on a rate 48% high cut more batches than needed.
Do, test first: the gene-call cost of a `virus` genome depends on its size class as well as its kind: a measured rate for giant-virus-sized genomes (state the length threshold from the data: the sample's genomes against `V1`'s and `S1`'s), leaving every estimate of the built stages (`S1`, `H1`, `V1` when built) within 20% of its run record and bringing `GV1`'s sample within 15%. No built stage's plan or published file changes (the plan hash of a stage already built is not recomputed).
Accept: the test fails first on the `GV1` sample (over 20%) and passes; the estimates of the built virus stages against their run records stay within 20%.
**Withdrawn 2026-11-20: the card's premise is wrong, and nothing needs changing before `GV1` is built** (a Sonnet agent read the root and the inputs read-only and stopped before editing, AGENTS §9; *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20, unless marked). (1) No length threshold separates giant viruses from the built virus stages: the largest genome of `S1` and `H1` is 735,411 bp and of `V1` 553,716 bp, while `GV1`'s median genome is 200,398 bp, and only 336 of its genomes (8.1% of its bases) are above 735,411 bp. A threshold there leaves the estimate at about 345 CPU-min (*derived*) against 236 from the sample; a lower one moves the plans of built stages. (2) The rate does not follow genome size: gv-meta ran at 4.20 s per Mbp on `S1` (median genome 45 kb), 3.78 on `H1` (249 kb), 3.72 on `V1` (25 kb) and 2.72 on the `GV1` sample (200 kb): `H1` and `GV1` have like sizes and rates 28% apart. What makes `GV1` cheaper is not known (contig mix, gene density); `GV1`'s genomes are multi-contig, median 11 contigs of median 9,215 bp. (3) `CALL_S_PER_MBP` is written into every stage's published gene-call plan (`rates.call_s_per_mbp`, hashed), so a new entry there would change the recorded plan of every later stage's siblings; a rate for one source would have to be a separate constant or a new genome kind, which is a design choice. **What stands instead**: `GV1` is built with the code's estimate (354 CPU-min, 2 batches). An estimate that is too high never stops a build (DEC-53) and only cuts one batch more than needed; the driver records the step against 236 CPU-min as the 2026-11-20 note says. `GV1`'s own run record will then give the rate on all 18,499 genomes, and a card to model it is written only if a later giant-virus source makes it matter.
